# Contradictions and collisions

What the inventoried artifacts give differently, found on 2026-10-22 for the Artifact Textbook (issue #120) and carried here with the status of each candidate. Two readers went over the inventory's numbers table (3,025 numbers from 69 artifacts; the data half and the performance half) for every quantity two artifacts give with different values; one reader per topic cluster went over the full inventory records for disagreements, revised figures, stale statements and shared unsourced claims. A script then looked for each candidate's quotes and values in the artifact's extracted text and, failing that, in the page's own files (its scripts and data), and the columns 'Quote A' and 'Quote B' below say whether they were found. A candidate is a reader's finding, not a verdict: 'yes' means the words are in the source as quoted, 'not found' that the reader paraphrased or erred, and the kind is the reader's classification until phase 2 reads both texts for each one. The reader's own classification of each number collision is given with it; the fifteen pairs a verifying reader did read before the sweep stopped carry its verdict in the 'Read' column. Of the 14 clusters, 3 were swept for disagreements in this pass; the rest are listed at the end and are swept in phase 2.

Nothing here is copied from memory: every value and quote is from the inventory's records, which were read from the artifact files. People are named by role; no address of any kind is reproduced.

The quotes, in the same order:

1. A, or-readiness-ledger: "98.2% exact level on whole C-ArmLSpine lateral frames, five-fold CV over 148 cases [95.7-100]" B, counting-from-the-sacrum: "\| Lateral, whole frame 141 cases, 617 vertebrae, 5-fold CV \| \| 98.22% \| \| 100% \|"
2. A, schwingnet-data-registry: "raw CBCT projections at quantised gantry angles, 301 XA frames each; rotational, not cranio-caudal" B, research-directions-from-the-data-holding-kcyckb: "case134 302-frame spin as the n 1 real check"
3. A, or-readiness-ledger: "98.2% exact level on whole C-ArmLSpine lateral frames, five-fold CV over 148 cases [95.7-100]" B, research-directions-from-the-data-holding-kcyckb: "it names 97.4 % of bodies on a whole lateral frame and 74.8 % with one body in view"
4. A, counting-from-the-sacrum: "\| Lateral, one vertebra in the field \| \| 72.85% \| \| 90.23% \|" B, handoff-schwingnet-ctspinopelvic1k-and-the-imaging-programme-rbcdmf: "97.4 % of bodies named on a whole lateral frame and 74.8 % with one body in view"
5. A, aidas-seed-grant-proposal: "A detector trained only on calibrated renders of DeepFluoro's own CTs finds the sacrum on about 96-98% of real frames (leave-one-specimen-out)." B, counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy: "A detector trained on renders of DeepFluoro's own CTs finds the sacrum on 90-97% of the real frames, six specimens held out in turn."
6. A, counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy: "71.67% for the same architecture trained on renders only." B, ninety-three-pixels: "the render-trained detector itself, at 0.740 recall against the film detector's 0.991"
7. A, fluoroscopy-data-atlas: "714 jpg (494/204/16), 698 annotated, 3,250 boxes; about 500 are BUU persons" B, spine-x-ray-data-handoff-bddbdd: "Images 716 lateral lumbar: 208 Honduran + 508 from BUU"
8. A, spine-x-ray-data-handoff-bddbdd: "BUU-LSPINE full release (3,600 patients)" B, state-of-the-literature-datasets-held-missing-size-and-power-8hg1ay: "BUU-LSPINE: 3,600 patients and 7,200 films, or 3,600 films from 1,200 patients; 7,204 films are on disk."

| 1 | variance explained with a per-frame smooth field | [Ninety-Three Pixels](../ninety-three-pixels/) | 92.3 (physics) / 93.9 (calibrated) | [Counting Levels Under Fluoroscopy](../counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy/) | 93.9% | Both give 93.9 for the calibrated model; what-is-left-after-physics gives per-frame 86.9-97.1 for four frames. Listed so a verifier can confirm which model 93.9 belongs to. | yes | yes | - |

The quotes, in the same order:

1. A, ninety-three-pixels: "takes physics to 92.3% and the calibrated model to 93.9%" B, counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy: "a per-frame smooth field (the device's window, scatter pedestal, heel, vignette) brings it to 93.9% against a quantum-noise ceiling of 99.5%"
2. A, schwingnet-data-registry: "16,531 digitised films without landmarks" B, handoff-schwingnet-ctspinopelvic1k-and-the-imaging-programme-rbcdmf: "with no written reason for the other 26,996 held films"
3. A, spine-program-decisions-qqhkqz: "Class-agnostic detection plus keypoints on any spine radiograph. mAP50 0.991, PCK@0.1 0.812." B, vertebra-detector-field-report-1jljc6: "\| NHANES II film, cervical \| \| 294 \| \| 0.970 \| \| 0.815 \| \| 0.924 \|"
4. A, imaging-data-holding-manifest-migration-and-index-fqmtjs: "374 scans, 355 patients; 10 VerSe'20 series never downloaded" B, fluoroscopy-data-atlas: "renders 9,612 over 801 CTSP cases; renders_verse 3,456 over 288; renders_ctrl 644 over 161"
5. A, state-of-the-literature-datasets-held-missing-size-and-power-8hg1ay: "render L6 recall 56.1 to 72.4 %, L6 named 44.9 % with no anchor on anomalous films, 49.9 % on anomalous film windows" B, naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh: "44.9% of errored windows carry one coherent offset across every vertebra-the P(B\|M?) ~ P(B?b?\|M?) degeneracy exactly as described-amounting to 8.3% of all isola"
6. A, naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh: "three-vertebra frames were named correctly 99.3% of the time. That is 76 spines and about 133 vertebrae" B, counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy: "from C2, three bodies of overlap, whole-spine CT \| \| 76 \| \| 834 / 834 \| \| 100%"
7. A, vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj: "the rare-variant image set holds 845 spines with a sixth lumbar vertebra, but those images contain no landmark" B, fluoroscopy-data-atlas: "synth_rare 12,929 renders over 2,844 cases (T13 86, L6 1,763), 9.0 GB; synth_rare_lowstrength 566; synth_v1 empty"
8. A, fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb: "Jecklin 2024 needed 3,317 pose-paired real frames from 10 cadavers to lift lumbar reconstruction from a surface score of 0.08 back to 0.63-0.73, still below its" B, lift-before-label-jzjq8t: "one case 8% against 76% on its DRR twin"
9. A, or-readiness-ledger: "TotalSegmentator and SPINEPS/VERIDAH agree on 818 of 900 vertebrae (90.9%; 58 disagree, 24 unmatched); 5 cases carry an L6 call and 13 a T13; the sacrum agrees" B, schwingnet-data-registry: "yes, lumbar, projected from the CBCT (two labellers agreeing on 814 of 910 bodies); 3 real L6 cases"
10. A, retrospective-fluoroscopy-protocol: "A second team member checks every frame of a random [to confirm: verification fraction of cases; 10% proposed] of cases" B, protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p: "100% of frames are inspected by a study team member before release"
11. A, vertebra-detector-field-report-1jljc6: "6.8 ms Inference / image" B, vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj: "roughly a quarter to half a second on a single ordinary CPU core"
12. A, or-readiness-ledger: "sacrum found 83.9%, vertebra recall 90.6% on whole frames once truth boxes use one convention" B, any-view-spine-plan: "Your record: 24 frames took detection from 80.5% to 84.0%."
13. A, or-readiness-ledger: "outline IoU 0.5 -> 90.6% at conf 0.25 (92.7% best); mutual-centre rule (convention-free) -> 90.9%" B, counting-from-the-sacrum: "is training now - 18,906 training frames, 56 test specimens"
14. A, overnight-the-honest-99-kantj8: "it returns a single region on 70.3% of vertebrae at 99.7% accuracy" B, or-readiness-ledger: "zero-shot level model + film keypoint detector \| \| COBRA lateral >=75 degrees \| \| population held out \| \| 599 \| \| 90.3 \| \| 86.1-93.9 \| \| 70.3"

16. A, counting-from-the-sacrum: "row (c) places an instrument frame on that chain and reads L2, correctly, with a margin of 0.18 over being one body off" B, schwingnet-figures-jshksq: "a sixth lumbar vertebra counted from the sacrum, margin 0.01 - honestly a weak one"

Canonical relations the reader checked:

- schwingnet-figures-jshksq superseded schwingnet-figures-xyz6kr: agrees. Same author and date (2026-09-22): jshksq is the 20:12 state ('the composite is not yet restacked', rows re-rendered to drop the algebraic rule, overlap mm not quotable), xyz6kr the 22:50 state ('the six figures, as built', page 1 of the compiled manuscript, S1-S4, Table 1 defects listed). xyz6kr's folder holds 13 of jshksq's row and audit images unreferenced, so it carries the earlier page's material. Neither page names the other, and xyz6kr still reports Figures 4 and 6 failed, so 'superseded' means later state, not finished set.
- vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj superseded naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc: agrees. Both 2026-09-18 by the same account with identical per-region rows; hjy4vc adds what a4rydj only previews: the sacrum detector (a4rydj: 'being given a detector of its own'), the majority rule (a4rydj: 'a tolerant version ... in testing ... eight points'; hjy4vc: 67.4 to 91.8), the widened cranial-anchor table (a4rydj's twelve thoracic studies), box repair and the 1,653-film comparison. But a4rydj keeps content hjy4vc drops (overlap and field tables with exposure counts, the threshold table, the sweep-worse-than-none result, the reader-study design and review-site addresses), so it is superseded as the headline and not as a record. Note also that the handoff of 2026-10-08 rules the v2/v6/v7-bank numbers both pages rest on record only.

Unsourced: The clinical motivation is asserted bare on the manuscript pages and sourced only in the outward documents: the 179 gold-fiducial placements over 38 months (naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh, naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc, vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj), half of surveyed surgeons having operated at the wrong level (hjy4vc, a4rydj, counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy, with the specialty changing between pages), 81% of events being a count gone wrong (counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy), 15 of 100 consecutive discectomies (hjy4vc, a4rydj), and 'a surgeon counts from whatever landmark is visible' (3dfzmh, the aidas proposal); the aidas proposal and level-counting-visual-abstract give Siminski 2023, Mody 2008, Furuya 2026 and Goodkin 2004, but the pages that will become the paper do not. Literature benchmarks are quoted without citation where they set the bar: 'best published registration-free naming is 73.3%' and 'published class-agnostic C-arm detection reaches 0.96-0.98' and the '28-point sim-to-real tax' (counting-from-the-sacrum), 'the best whole-spine identification in the literature is 96.5%, on MRI' (overnight-the-honest-99-kantj8), 69.6%/97.6% attributed only to 'published literature' (hjy4vc), and the 88.3% Huang figure that registration-free-level-id-feasibility-uramo1 says failed verification (hjy4vc, a4rydj, fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb). Prevalence priors are unsourced where used: L6 in roughly 2% of people (counting-against-two-anchors-qkktgu), Castellvi I as two-thirds or 68% of LSTV (overnight-99, research-directions), 'C1 is excluded from these datasets' (vertebral-count-anomaly-rule). Novelty is claimed by unrecorded searches: no published frame-chaining method (3dfzmh), no AP-to-lateral synthesis and 'lateral naming is already at the ceiling' (any-view-spine-plan), no lumbosacral 3-D classifier (research-directions), no evidence that navigation reduces wrong-level surgery (visual abstract, aidas). Operational figures shown to a surgeon and a funder are marked unverified by their own pages: cadaver work needing no IRB and fifteen business days (counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy, aidas), navigation capital of $1-2.5M (visual abstract), 'costs milliseconds' (hjy4vc), 'about 3% in ordinary use' (a4rydj), 'surgeons centre the field the same way' (or-readiness-ledger), 'the OR always has both AP and lateral views' (lift-before-label-jzjq8t), 'the count from the sacrum is undetermined in a limited field' (schwingnet-figures-xyz6kr).

Stale: Every member carries lines that have moved by 2026-10-22. The September-13 ladder (level-context-ladder-xd7eltc) names jobs 40215523/40215525 and 'none is fluoroscopy'; the September-15 research pages say 'nothing is stale' (registration-free-level-id-feasibility-uramo1), 'retrain 40285209 pending' and CUT v2 queued with all-spine v2 weights destroyed (fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb), and an adversarial verification pass that 'did not run' (lift-before-label-jzjq8t). The September-17/18 manuscript pages (naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh, naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc, vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj) say jobs 40321409 and 40321779 are running, 'when they land they replace this table', a 376-patient transitional cross-validation is running, and 'a verified search finished today'; the handoff of 2026-10-08 later rules the allspine v2, v6, v7, anchor_drr and vindrps numbers 'record only' and says nothing after 2026-09-27 changed the banks or models. The September-22 pages (counting-from-the-sacrum, or-readiness-ledger, both figures pages) list a fleet detector 'training now', the CTSpinoPelvic1K id-is-a-person audit running (answered 2026-10-08 as 301 of 801 persons on both sides), jobs 40411495/40411934 and device fits running, a sampler 'not adopted' pending A/B, the composite 'not yet restacked' at 20:12 and Figures 4 and 6 failed at 22:50. The undated design pages (counting-against-two-anchors-qkktgu: arms not run; vertebra-detector-field-report-1jljc6: CSXA behind a browser session, BUU 77 of 7,200 files, level head not started) describe states the ladder and handoff have passed. The outward documents are dated: the aidas proposal was due 2026-10-16 with '[to confirm]' placeholders and refiners 'being measured as arms'; counting-levels-under-fluoroscopy says 'start this week' and 'the manuscript in preparation'; the handoff records the disclosure submitted 2026-09-25 with the provisional filing not recorded, the CNS talk 31 Oct-4 Nov and paper 1 held for tech transfer. The newest pages (research-directions-from-the-data-holding-kcyckb, 2026-10-08: 'nothing ran on the grid', letters drafted and none sent, verified transitional test count 'zero today'; any-view-spine-plan, 2026-10-21: census PR #76 'goes to the grid next', issues #59-#72 open) are plans whose measurements are not yet recorded.

Notes: All 21 records were read; fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb was read to line 1053 of 1290 (the unread tail holds external_sources, cross_references, related_titles and notes; its summary, claims, numbers and stale markers were captured). No plain-text file was needed. Quotes are drawn from the records' excerpts; a few short quotes reproduce the record's own condensed form of a table row. Two rulings from later members bear on the clustering's canonical list (counting-from-the-sacrum, naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc, naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh, schwingnet-figures-xyz6kr, level-context-ladder-xd7eltc): the handoff of 2026-10-08 declares the allspine v2/v6/v7 banks leaked and their numbers record only, with the results catalog as the authority ('the catalog wins'), which covers the ladder and both naming pages; and any-view-spine-plan (2026-10-21) carries the newest C-ArmLSpine numbers (96.4%, 43.7% AP, 80.5/84.0 detection), so a reader of the canonical pages alone would cite superseded values. The cluster also holds two methods under one name (count from an anchor versus name from morphology) and the hybrid R + C = 22 rule, which the figures dropped the day the rule page was created. The clustering gave no duplicate relations; the duplicate-text candidates above are additional. People are named by role only; vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj lists three review-site addresses, one of which carries an account name resembling a person, recorded here only as an address and not reproduced. No e-mail, phone or home address appears in this output.

- counting-against-two-anchors-qkktgu canonical (clustering gives no superseded or duplicate relation) research-directions-from-the-data-holding-kcyckb: agrees. It is the only full statement of the anchor-ordinal design and research-directions (8 Oct 2026) carries it forward unchanged as CT-7, unrun; nothing in the cluster replaces it, though its corpus counts (sixteen lumbar ribs, the foramina table) are on an earlier label release than v11 and its T12/L1 0.990 is reinterpreted there.
- spinal-border-variants-fye5ef canonical (clustering gives no superseded or duplicate relation) naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh: agrees. It is the only page with the segmentation-derived co-occurrence table and the rib-12 bimodality; but it is not the programme's adopted co-occurrence number (the naming page's VerSe 3.61 is, and that page reports a CTSpinoPelvic1K both-cell of two), so canonical for its measurements, not for the odds ratio the roadmap uses.
- rib-anchor-audit-t7wr2h not superseded (clustering lists no superseded relation) spinal-border-variants-fye5ef: disagrees. The border page's renumbering table starts at the audit's v5 end state (109 ribs on the wrong vertebra, 16 misnumbered cases) and carries it to 19 and 2 on the same data/v5_final, so the audit's numbering verdict is superseded in part; its QC findings (truncation, paired exclusion) and its 2 of 22 radiologist agreement are not restated anywhere and remain canonical.
- openspine-board-t3gfds no superseded relation given spine-program-decisions-qqhkqz: disagrees. Both records state the decisions page supersedes the board (same decisions, corpus and licensing table, the IRB purge recorded as done where the board still asks for a go), and the two share passages verbatim; the clustering's empty superseded and duplicate lists miss this pair.
- fluorocap-overnight-report-xnhgdo no superseded relation given overnight-the-honest-99-kantj8: agrees. The two overnight pages are consecutive nights (15 Sep and 15 to 16 Sep) on the same pipeline and the records do not establish that one replaces the other; the honest-99 corpus counts post-date the report's, but the report's T13 and L6 render results are not restated, so no supersession is borne out.

Unsourced: The cluster's anchoring anatomy is asserted rather than cited. Counting Against Two Anchors and Spinal Border Variants both state the textbook rule that four pairs of sacral foramina are normal and five mean an absorbed lumbar segment, with no reference, and both then find the corpus's mode is three. Counting Against Two Anchors states that the ilium's auricular surface is not relinquished when a lumbar segment fuses above it, and the Rib Anchor Audit states that ilium contact with the L5 transverse process is sacralization by definition and that sacroiliac height is tight in normal anatomy and shifted by about a segment in transitional cases; neither measures it, and the nearest measurement (the border page's Castellvi span and gap proxies) is flat. Counting Against Two Anchors gives the L6 prevalence as roughly 2% and the lowest-lumbar-is-L5 prior as right 97% of the time without a source, calls the name-prediction defect 'the documented failure mode' without saying where, and says the CNN-over-Transformer finding came from organ segmentation without a citation. The Rib Anchor Audit asserts that thoracic bodies sit about 20 mm apart, that 29% is above and 1.9% is a plausible rate of rib asymmetry, and that counting from C2 on whole-spine imaging is the only definitive method and the reason LSTV is chronically mislabelled (the feasibility page cites Liebrand 2026 and Nakagawa 2017 for related statements, so this one is sourced only indirectly). Overnight: The Honest 99% and Research directions both put Castellvi type I at about two thirds or 68% of LSTV with no citation at the statement, and Overnight: The Honest 99% and State of the literature both quote the VerSe rare-class precedent (15 or 28 L6 training cases sufficing, T13 found by the counting prior) without naming the paper. OpenSpine Board and Spine Program Decisions share, unsourced, the sentence that the published LSTV state of the art is AUC 0.84 at 62.3% prevalence and the reading that BUU's 4.0% is a population rate; the feasibility page is the only member that names the study (Kwak 2025). Overnight: The Honest 99% says one published model attends to the twelfth rib and counts down from it, unnamed. Across the cluster, every variant count on CTSpinoPelvic1K (lumbar ribs 10, 13, 14, 16 or 25; L6 13, 16, 18, 22 or 29; four lumbar 7, 9 or 13; Castellvi 31, 33 or 34) is given as a fact of the corpus with no statement of which label release or counting rule produced it, so an experiment planner would take whichever page they read as established.

Stale: All three primary members are undated and carry status that has moved. Rib Anchor Audit says v5 exists only as a local tree at data/v5_final with nothing pushed to HuggingFace, 107 of 113 spine cases finalized, and its two sacral-boundary tests 'next' and uncomputed; Research directions (8 Oct 2026) has the label tree at v11 with v10 as the public mirror and the Handoff (8 Oct) has the dataset released on Zenodo and Hugging Face. Spinal Border Variants measures 'from data/v5_final' and lists seven cases that 'need a person' (0008, 0389, 0720, 0344, 0179, 0412, 1153) with no record of their resolution. Counting Against Two Anchors says the rib-bearing head is 'already built', the staged pipeline is the comparator 'that already exists', its five arms are to be run with arms 1 and 2 as the result, and its counts are 'measured on the released labels'; by 8 Oct the design is CT-7, ranked 14th with nothing run on the grid, and seven L6 records of the release are out of every truth pool pending re-read. Among the secondaries: LSTV Verification Bench is dated 15 September 2026, 'PubMed Central pass one', with counters at 0 of 0, and Overnight: The Honest 99% (16 Sep) lists the 585-figure bench and a 179-panel bench as waiting for the owner, while Research directions records FluoroCap dropped by directive with no 200-panel test set. FluoroCap Overnight Report (15 Sep) says nothing has taught the model a T13 and the corpus has no real T13 or L6 panel; the next day's table has 254 T13 and 338 L6 panels and the naming page (17 Sep) brings 78 T13 and 114 L6 vertebrae into bank v7. OpenSpine Board (11 Sep) marks BUU's LSTV truth 'unused' and lists purging the IRB files as waiting on the owner; Spine Program Decisions records the purge done and says a rare-class fold count is 'running now' and L6 Dice 0.00 is 'investigating', which Research directions closes as measured (Dice 0.000 with twelvefold duplication). Registration-Free Level ID Feasibility (15 Sep) finds no public intraoperative fluoroscopy set with level labels and says 'nothing is stale' as of that date; State of the literature (8 Oct) holds one (C-ArmLSpine) with no human transitional read, and Research directions and the Handoff both say the verified real-frame transitional test count is zero 'today' against 35 needed, with every data request drafted and none sent, 33 Castellvi grades against 71 to 142 expected, and the CNS meeting of 31 Oct to 4 Nov 2026 ahead. Research directions also notes the VerSe L6 count disagrees across files (85, 47, 50 or 87) and that the 2026-09-17 refutation of pretrain-then-fine-tune was never re-run on a clean bank.

Notes: Worked from the fourteen records in full and from the plain text of the three primaries, the honest-99 anomaly table, the naming page's co-occurrence paragraphs and the research-directions project rows; quotes from the other secondaries are the records' own. The decisive find is in the naming page's supplementary text (not in its record): on CTSpinoPelvic1K the co-occurrence both-cell was two (OR 4.00 [1.00, 16.03]) against the border page's 44 (OR 19.6) on the same 802 scans, which the naming page attributes to the abdominopelvic field not reaching the upper ribs; the two pages define a thoracolumbar variant differently (rib-12 length ratio versus label-voxel flags). The border page's '35 at four and 26 at six' labelled LSTV cases exceed its own 33 non-normal labels, and research-directions' CT-7 gate gives yet a third cell layout; I read the border page's 35 and 26 as all cases at those counts despite its wording. The audit's two lumbar-rib numbers (14 by strict incidence, 25 ribs on a lumbar vertebra) are in one artifact; I paired them with research-directions' 10 so the entry has two artifacts. The order between the undated border page and the naming page (17 Sep) is unknown, so 19.6 versus 3.61 is reported as a contradiction, not a supersession; the naming page's 'smaller than first tabulated' refers to its own all-rows 4.30. Dates: Research directions, State of the literature and the Handoff are all 8 Oct 2026 from the second account and repeat each other; where they agree they are one source. No e-mail, phone or address appears in the records; the grid login the board's header carries is not reproduced. The clustering's canonical set is reasonable, but its empty superseded list misses that the border page carries the audit's rib numbering forward (109 to 19) and that the decisions page supersedes the board.

The quotes, in the same order:

1. A: "The DRR at the registered pose is a by-product you get for free, but training on the real frame beats training on that DRR (4.89 vs 7.83 mm landmarks on DeepFluoro, Rapuri 2026 )." B: "DRRs with +/-2.5 degrees view augmentation were the best arm at every threshold ( 4.87 / 6.02 / 9.73 ). A generator wins where the detector is already confident and loses where it is not; physics plus pose augmentation wins everywhere."
2. A: "80.9% of a real frame's attenuation variance (NCC 0.899), same band shape in every specimen" B: "The stored DeepFluoro frames also turned out to be log-compressed display images; read correctly, physics alone explains 86% of a real frame rather than 80%, and the calibrated renderer about 90% of a held-out one."
3. A: "our renderer explains 80.9% of a real frame's variance; nine calibrated device terms add 4.3 points, held out by specimen; a per-frame smooth field reaches 93.9% against a 99.5% quantum-noise ceiling" B: "read correctly, physics alone explains 86% of a real frame rather than 80%, and the calibrated renderer about 90% of a held-out one."
4. A: "what is left is a smooth per-frame field with nothing local in it - 0.4% of variance at <=2 px - so there is nothing for a regional network to learn." B: "After the field, the 2-16 px bands sit at 0.1-0.8% of the frame's variance each - two to seven times the noise floor, not at it."
5. A: "The edge lines are the real frame being sharper than the render: the CT is 1 mm voxels magnified 1.5x onto 0.19 mm pixels, and a render cannot hold detail the CT never had. The bowel mottle is gas that moved between the CT and the fluoroscopy." B: "the border ring, a fifth of the field, holds about two thirds of the residual, because the real frame falls off through the penumbra and the render stops at a hard edge; inside it, cortical edges agree with the real frame to within a few grey levels at every bone thickness. A thin-bone deficit re..."
6. A: "A detector trained on renders of DeepFluoro's own CTs finds the sacrum on 90-97% of the real frames, six specimens held out in turn." B: "A detector trained only on calibrated renders of DeepFluoro's own CTs finds the sacrum on about 96-98% of real frames (leave-one-specimen-out)."
7. A: "The clean render-only number for that specimen is 88.5-91.3% at conf 0.05 (three seeds)." B: "a detector trained only on calibrated renders of that dataset's own CTs finds the sacrum on about 96-98% of real frames, but trained on renders of other living whole-body CTs it finds it on about 64-77%"
8. A: "finds the sacrum on 76.9% of 365 real frames pooled over all six specimens (by specimen 47.8-96.2%)" B: "The manuscript's 76.9% belongs to the anchor_none_r2 generation; the v2 generation every real fine-tune started from pools to 64.0%."
9. A: "Scatter width boxed to 6-16 px: +4.3 , 15.8 px with spread 0.4, every term stable - the calibration now applied." B: "\| Siemens Cios Fusion (DeepFluoro) \| \| 366 \| \| 0.899 -> 0.905 (+1.3) \| \| 0.24 \| \| 0.67 \| \| 0.20 / 2.2 \| \| -0.08 \| \| 3.6 (2.9) \| \| rect blades, 98% of frame \|"
10. A: "So it was calibrated , not learned, and it transfers to whole-spine CTs because every fitted term belongs to" B: "Real-vs-styled distances on the Cios card: NPS 0.111 against a real-vs-real floor of 0.015; grey histogram 3.1 vs 0.14"

Canonical relations the reader checked:

- ninety-three-pixels superseded what-is-left-after-physics: agrees. What Is Left After Physics explicitly revises ninety-three's residual claim ('nothing local' was too strong; the 2-16 px bands sit at two to seven times the noise floor) and keeps the same calibrated renderer, so for the residual decomposition the supersession holds. It is partial: what-is-left is undated and carries none of ninety-three's other content (the 836-to-139 px downsampling ladder, the +4.3/+4.1 device-term gain, scatter width 15.8 px, the 597-frame detector and its 76.9% and 26-point spread, the C2 finding, the re-render decision), and Counting Levels Under Fluoroscopy (runs of 21 September) supersedes both on the variance figures (86% physics, ~90% held-out, border ring holds two thirds of the residual, thin-bone deficit withdrawn). A planner should read ninety-three for calibration and resolution, what-is-left for the residual, and counting-levels for the corrected numbers.

Unsourced: The cluster asserts several things with no source or measurement in any page. The comparators for the project's own numbers are the weakest: Counting From The Sacrum gives 'the best published registration-free naming is 73.3%' and 'published class-agnostic C-arm detection reaches 0.96-0.98' with no citation, and says the 28-point sim-to-real tax 'matches what the field reports' without naming the field; Ninety-Three Pixels says the opposite ('no per-vertebra recall on real fluoroscopy is published at all') also without a search record on the page. Both Ninety-Three Pixels and Counting From The Sacrum state that LevelCheck's 100% imports identity from the patient's own CT by registration without citing it (Lift Before Label, Any-View Spine Plan and the Reality Check do cite Lo 2015 and De Silva 2016). The AIDaS proposal and the Retrospective Fluoroscopy Protocol share the inference that 'synthetic fluoroscopy from CT is close in physics but tuned to one C-arm and one cadaveric population', drawn from DeepFluoro alone before any second device was fitted. Lift Before Label asserts that the renderer 'is already at the DeepDRR/SyntheX level', that 'the OR always has both' AP and lateral views, that registration-derived labels are biased toward registration-friendly frames, and that the level's discriminating cues are projected rather than depth-ambiguous features; none is measured anywhere in the cluster, and the first is contradicted by the sampler audit and the ledger's styling distances. Ninety-Three Pixels asserts that 'vertebral bodies are large, low-frequency objects and the detector does not need the pixels' as the reading of a flat ladder, which the Level Context Ladder's 48-to-96 px gain and the Reality Check's 2.6x sharpness gap qualify for naming. Any-View says 'renders add unlimited exact pairs' and Lift says the registered DRR 'is a by-product you get for free'; neither costs rendering or registration. What Is Left After Physics asserts that bowel gas 'is not a function of the CT' and that 'YOLO11 is BatchNorm' without reference. The OR Readiness Ledger asserts that 'surgeons centre the field the same way every time' and that 'the crops are upscaled windows, so a detector ... loses most of them' as explanations rather than measurements. The data registry's 'only' claims (the only confirmed lumbar cine, the only large real thoracic fluoroscopy without a DUA, the only voxel-anchored C1/C2/C7 source) and the Reality Check's unverified device-frame figures (OEC, Ziehm native frame sizes) are search summaries, not verified facts.

Stale: Every primary page is a dated snapshot with open work. Ninety-Three Pixels (21 Sep 2026, 03:3x EDT) has eight GPU arms live, dr_eval queued, DeepFluoro 'acquired, not yet inspected', two manuscript word counts (6,299 and 7,244) and the re-render decision left to the owner; Any-View Spine Plan (21 Oct) shows the renderer question still open (ablation #69). What Is Left After Physics (undated, 2026) says recall holds are 'being measured directly right now' and three direct tests 'are next', one of which (the Fourier low-band swap) Counting Levels Under Fluoroscopy already reports from the 21 September runs. Lift Before Label (15 Sep) never ran its verification pass ('scheduled to run when the limit resets'), lists the registration harness as running and Protocol 1's frames as 'still to come'; the Retrospective Fluoroscopy Protocol is still 'Date Submitted: Draft' with a January 2027 target and dozens of [to confirm] fields, and the AIDaS proposal's 16 October 2026 deadline has passed with the PI slot unfilled and refiner results 'pending'. The OR Readiness Ledger (22 Sep 06:10, re-scored 06:50) has the clean DeepFluoro retrain (job 40411934), crop re-scores, device fits (jobs 40411903-07, 40412003, 40412010), the SpineSAC inventory, the device census and the multi-device corpus chain (renders 40411951/53/55, trainings 40411961-66) all running or queued, 'no styled corpus exists', and the whole-spine curves still on the 312-box denominator. Counting From The Sacrum (22 Sep) has the fleet-wide detector training (18,906 frames), the sampler A/B running, the CTSpinoPelvic1K id audit running and figure row (d) rendering; SchwingNet Figures (22 Sep 22:50) has Figures 3, 4 and 6 and Table 1 being fixed and the integration pass running. The FluoroCap pages are a 14-15 September snapshot: the Pipeline Map's FluoroGen loop was dropped the next morning, the Overnight Report's refetch round two, v2 retrain (registered 08:13), CUT v2 (jobs 40285316/7) and Instagram/YouTube harvests were running, and the Reality Check's v2 retrain (40285209) and translator (40285274/5) were pending with human_qc_status 'pending' on all 17,486 manifest rows; by 20 October the Data Registry rules FluoroCap out of training (directive D1). The Level Context Ladder (13 Sep 11:30) has the +BUU rungs and VinDr pseudo-labelling relaunched (jobs 40215523, 40215525); the Vertebra Detector Field Report is undated with the level head 'not started' and the ignore mask 'not yet in this run', both overtaken. The Data Registry (20 Oct) lists fourteen video-source requests drafted in September and never sent, COBRA Varian geometry solved 23 September with no bank built, 175 COBRA cases embargoed to 2032-03-01, VinDr's IRB determination pending, the BUU licence unread, case39 unexported, LUMOS never fetched and RSNA 2022 never unpacked; Any-View (21 Oct) has the census (PR #76) about to go to the grid, IPCAI 2025 'not yet', and everything confidential before the provisional (by 30 October) and the talk of 2 November 2026.

Notes: Records and texts were read whole for all three primary members and all fourteen secondary members; plain texts were consulted for the ledger's device-card table, ninety-three's GAN and residual sentences, counting-levels' log-compression and border-ring correction, what-is-left's Rapuri reading, lift's LevelCheck and Pelphix lines, any-view's detection table and the reality check's corrected literature figures. Two cautions for the planner: the clustering's canonical choice of Lift Before Label rests on a page whose verification pass never ran and whose Jecklin, Spine-DART and Pelphix numbers the same-day Reality Check corrected; and the cluster's answer 'registered real frames beat their DRR twins' is one reading of Rapuri 2026 Table 1 (real 4.89 vs unaugmented DRR 7.83) while What Is Left After Physics reads the same table as pose-augmented DRRs (4.87) matching or beating real frames at every threshold. The numbers most often re-quoted downstream (80.9% physics, 93.9% smooth field, 96-98% population-matched sacrum, +4.3 device terms, 76.9% pooled sacrum) each have a later correction in the cluster (86% and ~90% held-out in Counting Levels; 88.5-91.3% clean and 64.0% v2 in the Ledger; +1.3 on the device card) that the October grant and IRB pages do not carry. Candidates about dataset counts (C-ArmLSpine 141/148/149 cases, TotalSegmentator 156/142 whole spines, CTSpinoPelvic1K 801/802, FluoroCap 19,048/19,581/29,484/29,500 panels) are included because the render and test arms are sized from them. No e-mail address, phone number or home address is reproduced; people are named by role.
